=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and a positive integer k.
The value of a sequence seq of size 2 * x is defined as:

(seq[0] OR seq[1] OR ... OR seq[x - 1]) XOR (seq[x] OR seq[x + 1] OR ... OR seq[2 * x - 1]).

Return the maximum value of any subsequence of nums having size 2 * k.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [2,6,7], k = 1
Output: 5
Explanation:
The subsequence [2, 7] has the maximum value of 2 XOR 7 = 5.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [4,2,5,6,7], k = 2
Output: 2
Explanation:
The subsequence [4, 5, 6, 7] has the maximum value of (4 OR 5) XOR (6 OR 7) = 2.

 
Constraints:

2 <= nums.length <= 400
1 <= nums[i] < 2^7
1 <= k <= nums.length / 2

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxValue(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

```
def max_xor(mask1, mask2):
    # maybe if mask1==0 or mask2==0 return 0
    best=0
    # iterate set bits of mask1
    m1 = mask1
    while m1:
        lsb1 = m1 & -m1
        a = lsb1.bit_length() - 1
        m2 = mask2
        while m2:
            lsb2 = m2 & -m2
            b = lsb2.bit_length() - 1
            val = a ^ b
            if val > best: best = val
            m2 ^= lsb2
        m1 ^= lsb1
    return best
```

Need maybe use `List` import. final code:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxValue(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        MAXV = 1 << 7

        def apply_or(bits: int, v: int) -> int:
            res = 0
            while bits:
                lsb = bits & -bits
                m = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                res |= 1 << (m | v)
                bits ^= lsb
            return res

        left = [0]*(n+1)
        dp = [0]*(k+1)
        dp[0] = 1
        for i, v in enumerate(nums):
            for cnt in range(k-1, -1, -1):
                bits = dp[cnt]
                if bits:
                    dp[cnt+1] |= apply_or(bits, v)
            left[i+1] = dp[k]

        right = [0]*(n+1)
        dp = [0]*(k+1)
        dp[0] = 1
        for i in range(n-1, -1, -1):
            v = nums[i]
            for cnt in range(k-1, -1, -1):
                bits = dp[cnt]
                if bits:
                    dp[cnt+1] |= apply_or(bits, v)
            right[i] = dp[k]

        ans = 0
        for c in range(k, n-k+1):
            lm = left[c]
            rm = right[c]
            if lm == 0 or rm == 0:
                continue
            # compute max xor
            bits1 = lm
            while bits1:
                lsb1 = bits1 & -bits1
                a = lsb1.bit_length() - 1
                bits2 = rm
                while bits2:
                    lsb2 = bits2 & -bits2
                    b = lsb2.bit_length() - 1
                    val = a ^ b
                    if val > ans:
                        ans = val
                    bits2 ^= lsb2
                bits1 ^= lsb1
        return ans
```

def add_or(bits, v):
    res=0
    while bits:
       lsb = bits & -bits
       res |= 1 << ((lsb.bit_length()-1)|v)
       bits ^= lsb
    return res

Let's maybe write final code with helper function inside method:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxValue(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)

        def add_or(bits: int, v: int) -> int:
            res = 0
            while bits:
                lsb = bits & -bits
                res |= 1 << ((lsb.bit_length() - 1) | v)
                bits ^= lsb
            return res

        left = [0] * (n + 1)
        dp = [0] * (k + 1)
        dp[0] = 1
        for i, v in enumerate(nums):
            for cnt in range(k - 1, -1, -1):
                if dp[cnt]:
                    dp[cnt + 1] |= add_or(dp[cnt], v)
            left[i + 1] = dp[k]

        right = [0] * (n + 1)
        dp = [0] * (k + 1)
        dp[0] = 1
        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            v = nums[i]
            for cnt in range(k - 1, -1, -1):
                if dp[cnt]:
                    dp[cnt + 1] |= add_or(dp[cnt], v)
            right[i] = dp[k]

        ans = 0
        bit = [1 << i for i in range(128)]
        for c in range(k, n - k + 1):
            lm = left[c]
            rm = right[c]
            if lm == 0 or rm == 0:
                continue
            for a in range(128):
                if lm & bit[a]:
                    for b in range(128):
                        if rm & bit[b]:
                            val = a ^ b
                            if val > ans:
                                ans = val
                                if ans == 127:
                                    return 127
        return ans
```
Need check if `dp[cnt]` is nonzero, but `add_or(dp[cnt], v)` uses dp[cnt] maybe modified? We pass int. Good.

No, cnt=c-1 processed later. So safe. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a possible issue with "maximum XOR between masks" using `lm
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
through the requested rhythm and finalize with code.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxValue(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)

        def add_or(bits: int, v: int) -> int:
            res = 0
            while bits:
                lsb = bits & -bits
                m = lsb.bit_length() - 1
                res |= 1 << (m | v)
                bits ^= lsb
            return res

        # left[i] = possible OR values using exactly k elements from nums[:i]
        left = [0] * (n + 1)
        dp = [0] * (k + 1)
        dp[0] = 1

        for i, v in enumerate(nums):
            for cnt in range(k - 1, -1, -1):
                if dp[cnt]:
                    dp[cnt + 1] |= add_or(dp[cnt], v)
            left[i + 1] = dp[k]

        # right[i] = possible OR values using exactly k elements from nums[i:]
        right = [0] * (n + 1)
        dp = [0] * (k + 1)
        dp[0] = 1

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            v = nums[i]
            for cnt in range(k - 1, -1, -1):
                if dp[cnt]:
                    dp[cnt + 1] |= add_or(dp[cnt], v)
            right[i] = dp[k]

        ans = 0
        bit = [1 << i for i in range(128)]

        for c in range(k, n - k + 1):
            lm = left[c]
            rm = right[c]
            if lm == 0 or rm == 0:
                continue

            for a in range(128):
                if lm & bit[a]:
                    for b in range(128):
                        if rm & bit[b]:
                            val = a ^ b
                            if val > ans:
                                ans = val
                                if ans == 127:
                                    return 127

        return ans
```
